We can use projected gradient/multiplicative updates to balance conv coefficients.

This problem resembles load balancing: Each pair (i,j) contributes a_i a_j to bucket i+j. We want bucket loads equal (S^2/(2n-1)). Updating a_i affects loads of buckets i+j with weights a_j. If a bucket is overloaded, we should decrease a_i in proportion to contributions to overloaded buckets. This is exactly gradient: overloaded coefficients pull down a_i.

Gradient via cross-correlation:
```
def grad_from_weights(a,w):
  n=len(a); L=2*n-1
  N = next_pow2(3*n-2)
  ar = a[::-1]
  fa = rfft(ar,N); fw = rfft(w,N)
  conv = irfft(fa*fw,N)
  return 2*conv[n-1:n-1+n]
```
But w length L may contain nan/inf? we ensure. `rfft` of large w each iter. Could precompute? w changes. Fine.

Let's attempt to design discrete sequences via solving a high-dimensional nonconvex optimization. Multiplicative gradient likely can find near-optimal continuous limit patterns. We can also use continuous optimization on a grid and then use that as start.

Let's think of continuous Euler-Lagrange numerically. We want choose f≥0, ∫=1 to minimize max h. Use method of alternating constraints: Start f uniform. To reduce peaks where h > M, decrease f at positions contributing to those peaks. This is same multiplicative update. In continuous, with smooth f parameterization, gradient of ∑ active h(t) wrt f(x)=2∑_{t active} f(t-x). This is cross-correlation. The update f ← f * exp(-η g(x)) normalized. This is an exponential tilt; it is exactly what our discrete log update will do.

So likely a simple projected multiplicative gradient with active set can approach optimum. Need tune.

Potential issues:
- Objective has many local minima? But maybe landscape smooth if use smooth weights.
- The objective is scale invariant; normalization after each step.
- Nonsmooth maxima; using softmax smooths.
- We can enforce symmetry to avoid orientation local minima.

Let's formulate with sum normalization S=1 for continuous-like, but in discrete score=2n*max(c). For S=n, score=2 max(c)/n. Easier to observe max ~0.75n. Update weights independent of scale if normalized. Use a sum = n.

Algorithm `optimize_multiplicative(a, budget_s, mode_smooth, sym, seed)`:
- Input a positive length n, normalize sum n.
- If sym, use half representation. But for simplicity first implement full with optional symmetrize at step (a = (a+a[::-1])/2). If symmetrize, length variables full but half determined; no need half log. Just a full symmetric. After update, average with reverse. This halves effective variability and ensures palindrome. Could also allow asym later.
- Maintain `eta` step size, `tau` temperature for softmax over c.
- Each iteration:
  1. c = conv(a,a) (FFT). max_c = c.max(); score = 2*max_c/n (if sum n).
  2. Weights w = softmax(beta*(c - c_max)) maybe add small floor to all to avoid zeros. Or alternative: w = (max(0, c - q)^α) for smooth top. Softmax over 2n-1 with beta maybe 1-20.
     Since c up to n, beta=1 maybe weights not too peaked. We can set beta adaptively: target weight on top fraction; or use top-K for sharper.
  3. grad = 2 * cross_corr(a,w) (full length n). Since w sums 1? If w normalized, scale irrelevant.
  4. If sym: grad = 0.5*(grad + grad[::-1]) because a sym? For symmetric a, gradient of symmetric objective with respect to symmetric variables? If we fix a_i=a_{n-1-i}, derivative wrt y_i = ∂/∂a_i + ∂/∂a_{n-1-i}. So grad_sym = grad + grad[::-1]. We'll divide by 2 maybe.
  5. Center grad: gc = grad - mean(grad) (or mean over updated half). Optional normalize by std.
  6. Update a *= exp(-eta * gc * step_scale). Clip to [0, 1000]. Then normalize sum n. If sym, sym again.
  7. Evaluate new c/score. If improved best. Accept if score <= current*(1+tol) else reduce eta (anneal maybe). Use simulated annealing: accept worse by temp to escape.
- Adaptive eta: start around 0.5? Since gc can be O(n?) Let's compute scale. If w normalized, cross_corr sum_i grad_i? ∑_i 2∑_j a_j w_{i+j}. ∑_i over all =2∑_j a_j wtot =1? if w sum=1 -> 2∑a=2n. Maybe a robust local search can combine:
1. Compute c and score.
2. Choose weights w = (c - τ)_+^γ, normalized. τ at e.g. 0.5*max or quantile.
3. D = 2 cross_corr(a,w) + eps.
4. Update: a_new = a * (mean(D)/D)^β (β small e.g. 0.2) or exp(-β*(log(D/mean))). This equalizes D.
5. Optionally blend with old a: a = (1-t)*a + t*a_new? In log domain better.

Let's design a high-level search with multiple metaheuristics:

Local optimizer mode:
Let's design `local_optimize(a, budget_s, seed, mode=0, sym=True)`:
```
a = symmetrize if sym, normalize sum n.
c=conv; score=fast_score; best=a; best_score.
current=a; current_score=score.
eta=0.5? inverse gamma=0.2.
mom? beta=0.5.
temp=0.005.
stagnant=0; it=0; t0.
while time<deadline:
  update depending mode:
    c=conv(a)
    w = weights(c, mode, state) # softmax or top-quantile
    D = cross_corr(a,w) # positive
    if mode == exp:
       gc = D - mean(D); gc = (gc - ?)/norm
       a_new = a * np.exp(-eta*gc)
    elif mode == inv:
       r = np.maximum(D, small)
       # damp: a_new = a * (mean(r)/r)^gamma
       # if D zero -> r=s; OK.
    elif mixed: combine exp and inv.
  symmetrize if sym.
  normalize.
  c_new=conv; score_new
  if score_new < best_score: best...
  accept logic: if score_new <= current_score + temp*current_score + 1e-9: accept, lower temp; else revert/reduce gamma.
  if stagnant: perturb with random smooth; maybe mode switch.
```

Let's design local optimizers in detail.

Function `make_weights(c, mode, state)`:
- Input c 1D length L.
- If `mode == 'soft'`: beta = state['beta']; m=c.max(); w = np.exp(beta*(c - m)); w += 1e-12; w/=w.sum();
- If `mode == 'quant'`: frac = state['frac']; p = state['pow']; if frac<=0? idx = argpartition(c, -K); K=max(1,min(L,int(frac*L))); thr=c[sorted?]. To avoid sorting full, use `np.partition(c, L-K)[L-K]`. w=np.maximum(c-thr,0)**p; if sum< small: w[idx]=1; w/=sum. For p=1 or 2.
- If `mode == 'upper'`: thr = max(mean(c), 0.9*max(c)?); w=(c-thr)_+; if zero use top one.
State can adapt beta/frac.

But for coordinate optimizer inverse update, weights need sum 1. Good.

Update functions:
```
def update_inv(a,w,D,state):
    gamma=state['gamma']
    D_rel = D / max(mean(D), tiny)
    # Maybe use rank: a_new = a * D_rel**(-gamma)
    logfactor = -gamma * np.log(np.maximum(D_rel, 1e-8))
    logfactor -= np.mean(logfactor) # optional to reduce scale? normalization handles, but can reduce dynamic range.
    logfactor = np.clip(logfactor, -5,5)
    a_new = a * np.exp(logfactor)
```
If D_rel >1, decrease; <1 increase. D positive. If gamma small, smooth. We can add sign? Not.

Exp update:
```
g = D - mean(D)
g = g / np.max(np.abs(g))
logf = -eta * g
logf = clip(logf, -3,3)
a_new = a*exp(logf)
```
Which is better? Inverse directly equalizes D, likely good. We'll use inverse as primary. Could incorporate momentum: maintain `mom_log` for logfactor, update `mom = beta*mom + (1-beta)*logf`, a*=exp(step*mom). But inverse update factor itself; momentum can smooth. Use small beta 0.3.

Acceptance: Use deterministic hill-climb with simulated acceptance. At each iteration, we compute candidate score. Maintain `current` (best recent accepted) and `best`. Accept if candidate score <= current score + temp. Temperature decay. If rejected, reduce gamma/eta and revert to current. If accepted worse (temperature), current becomes cand; best unchanged. Encourage exploration.

Stagnation: If no best improvement for `stagnant` iterations (e.g. 200), perturb current:
- multiplicative random low-freq: `a *= exp(∑ rng.cos(...))`, normalize, sym. If score too bad > best*1.2, revert to best? We can use current=best and temp increase.
- Or mix with random start: `a = 0.5*best + 0.5*random` normalized.
- Also switch mode (soft/quant) or gamma.

Now, possible insight: The problem of minimizing max coefficient of P^2 can be attacked by choosing P to approximate a square root of the all-ones polynomial. There are known "frequency sampling" methods: Choose desired coefficients c_k = desired profile (flat), take DFT, square-root its Fourier transform, inverse DFT, take absolute/square? We can choose C flat with boundary taper, compute its DFT: C_hat(ω)=∑ c_k e^{-i k ω}=P(e^{iω})^2. We want to find P with nonnegative coefficients. If C_hat(ω) is nonnegative? Not necessarily. Let's consider using polynomial root/PCM: We want P^2 flat; choose P as a nonnegative "lowpass" with many zeros on unit circle to make P^2 magnitude small? But P nonnegative means P(1)>0. The coefficient max relates to L1 norm of P^2; by Parseval, ∑ c_k^2 = (1/2π)∫ |P(e^{iω})|^4 dω. If c_k bounded by M and length L, then ∑ c_k^2 ≥ S^4/L = n^4/(2n) (if S=n), so M^2 ≥ n^4/(2n) -> M ≥ n^2/√2? impossible (M O(n)). Wait Parseval for coefficients of P^2: ∑ |c_k|^2 = (1/2π)∫ |P^2|^2 = (2π)∫ |P|^4 /? P is polynomial degree n-1. If all c_k ≤M and L≈2n, then ∑ c^2≤M^2 2n. Also total sum c=S^2=n^2 -> by Cauchy M≥n^2/L=n^2/(2n)=n/2, score≥1. L2 bound matches lower bound. Equality if c flat. Good.

We can use L2 norm to guide: We want c close to constant. Inverse update of D equalizes first-order; maybe also minimize variance of c. Could add variance penalty: minimize objective max + μ Var(c) or sum exp. Softmax with β moderate approximates sumexp, not variance. Maybe implement `smooth_max` as logsumexp β. This smooth objective gradient naturally reduces all high coefficients. Use β schedule increasing. This may be smoother than top quantile. We can use logsumexp objective for low-dim/coordinate and use gradient D with softmax weights. In inverse update, D from softmax. If β high, top focused. Good.

Let's use primary update: weights = softmax(beta*c). D = cross_corr(a, w). Update a_new = a * (mean(D)/D)^gamma (inverse). This pulls D toward mean; since D = gradient of weighted sum c. For softmax, D_i ~ expected a_{k-i}. Equalizing D may reduce weighted sum and max.

Could also use D itself as a load to update? Another natural balancing update: c_k should be flat; pairs (i,j) contribute to c_k. For each i, the "responsibility" R_i = ∑_j a_j b_{i+j}, where b_k is indicator of c_k being high. Decrease a_i if R_i high. That's inverse.

Need choose beta. c values O(n). If beta=1, softmax weights spread; beta=5 peaks. We can adapt beta based on current score: if score just above target, use high beta (top few); if far, lower. In local optimizer, we can run a schedule: beta = 1 + it*0.001 up to 10; gamma = 0.15; occasionally gamma increase. Or use quantile top 20% to not overfocus only one coefficient.

Let's implement a flexible `local_optimize` where `mode` integer:
- mode 0: inverse soft exponent: weights = top-quantile with gamma; update inverse.
- mode 1: exp gradient centered.
- mode 2: inverse with softmax, beta ramp.

Helper `get_parent()`:
```
def get_parent_sequence():
    try:
       p=globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
       if p is not None and len(p)>0:
          a=np.asarray(p,dtype=float)
          a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a,0.0)
          a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
          if a.sum()>0:
             return normalize_sequence(a, float(len(a)))
    except: pass
    return None
```

- kind 1: block profile: random cut points sorted, assign random values, maybe one zero block. Sym by average.
- kind 2: polynomial in u=2t-1: random coeffs c_p for p=0..d; a=exp(∑ c_p u^p). Sym ensures even? If odd powers anti, but sym will make even. Use c for even p; or after sym okay.
- kind 3: U/V shaped: `shape = 1 + α*|u|^p`; a=shape + small, or exp. α random [-2,3], p random [0.5,4]. This covers center peak/U.
- kind 4: endpoints heavy: `a=1+α/exp?` Use `a = 1 + α / (eps + |u|)^p` sym? This peaks endpoints if |u| max1; center small. Also center heavy `a = 1 + α / (eps + (1-|u|)^p)`.
- kind 5: sinusoidal squared: a = 1 + α cos^2(π m t) etc.

- `beta_power`: a = x^p + ε(1-x)^q with x=(i+0.5)/n.
- `sine edge`: a=1+α/√(x + eps) etc.

Normalize to sum n.

Local optimizer with inverse update should handle endpoint singular. Need ensure values not clipped to zero: The update can make endpoints huge? D at endpoints maybe? For one-sided sqrt, top coefficients central plateau; D_i? Could refine.

Let's think about acceptable runtime and exact evaluation. `evaluate_sequence` uses `np.convolve`, which for Python list of length maybe 2048 takes maybe? But if our returned sequence length 512, exact fast. We should return the best candidate exact among top. If search at 2048 improves, exact validation length 2048 okay; but np.convolve O(4M) okay. We need ensure time: If exact evaluating 5 candidates length 2048, maybe <1s each? Let's check C double loop 4M ~0.02s? Fine. For 4096 ~16M ~0.1s. OK. Use exact top 6.

Now, potential `np.convolve` for lists may be O(n^2) Python? It's in C, but direct algorithm maybe double loop in C. 16M operations maybe 0.1s. Fine.

Let's refine local optimizer with continuous endpoint insight. We need avoid local minima. We can run a "metro-polis" where updates are inverses with weights. Let's develop a robust algorithm in code.

Detailed `local_optimize` design:
Inputs: a (float array length n), budget_s, seed, mode (0 inverse, 1 exp), sym (bool), use_lp flag.
Steps:
- Normalize to sum n. If desired sym, sym.
- State: c, score. best, current=best.
- `gama` for inverse: start 0.08; `eta` for exp:0.3; `beta` for softmax start maybe 1; `quant` fractional if quant mode.
- Momentum arrays? We'll update log_adjust directly with momentum.
```
yt = np.zeros(n) # moment log multiplier
```
At iteration:
  `c=fast_conv(current)`; update current_score? It should be from previous.
  Choose weights:
    If mode==0 (quant-inverse):
       frac maybe 0.25 + 0.25*(1 - score/target?)? Use top 30% coefficients. `K=max(1,int(L*frac))`; thr=np.partition(c, L-K)[L-K]; w=np.maximum(c-thr,0); if sum small take top K indices; w/=sum.
       (Could use pow=1; if c-thr small, ok.)
       D=cross_corr(current,w)
       D_rel=D/mean(D)
       mult = (D_rel)**(-gamma)
    elif mode==1 (softmax-inverse):
       beta = beta0 + stretch * it? or adapt.
       w=softmax(beta*(c-c.max()))
       D=cross_corr(...)
       D_rel = D/mean(D)
       mult=(D_rel)**(-gamma)
    else exp:
       w=softmax...; D; g=(D-mean)/maxabs; mult=exp(-eta*g)
  Sym before/after: if sym, mult=sym(mult), current=sym(current).
  Apply momentum: `log_adj = np.log(np.maximum(mult, tiny))`; `log_adj -= mean(log_adj)` maybe; `yt=0.7*yt+0.3*log_adj`; `cand=current*np.exp(clip(yt, -5,5))`.
  Normalize; sym; if max>1000: scale? Actually sum n; if max>1e6 clip.
  Evaluate cand.
  If cand score finite:
    if cand < best: best, temp?; stagnant=0
    accept if cand <= current + temp*current + 1e-12: current=cand; temp*=0.995; if candidate improved current reduce gamma? else...
    else: current unchanged; gamma*=0.8 or eta*0.8; yt*=0.5; stagnant+=1
  If stagnant > 100:
    # increase exploration: apply random smooth perturbation to current (or best)
    if time: perturb; if score bad revert to best; temp*=1.5; gamma*=0.7; yt=...
  If time for LP and n<=1024 and not used: g=lp_improve(current,...); evaluate; blend; if better accept; used.
  Iteration count check; maybe every 200 iterations recompute beta/frac:
     frac = max(0.05, 0.5 - 0.01*improvement?) Use dynamic: If score high >target+0.1, frac=0.5; if close, frac=0.1.
```

But using inverse mult=(D_rel)^(-gamma) can cause strong changes if D_rel varies. Let's check scale. D_rel maybe O(1). gamma=0.1 gives 10% change. Momentum smooth. Good.

Should we center mult by subtracting mean? Normalization handles global scale, so centering not necessary, but can reduce numeric. `log_adj -= mean(log_adj)` before exp to keep product ~1, but normalization after anyway. Centering might alter relative changes? Subtract mean only changes global scale, no relative effect. OK.

Temp acceptance: We need avoid getting stuck. Start current=best. `temp=0.002` relative. Accept if cand_score <= current_score*(1+temp) + 1e-10. If accept worse, current_score increases; best unchanged. Then try to recover. This is simulated annealing. But after many iterations, current might be worse than best; we can reset current to best periodically. Use if score current > best*1.02 and stagnant > 50: current=best.copy(); recompute.

Perturbation:

z=np.zeros(n)
for m in 1..5: amp=0.3/m; z += amp*cos(2π m t + phase)
cand=current*np.exp(z)
# also random endpoint boost: cand[:n//10] *= exp(rng.normal)
normalize, sym optional, score; if score<best*1.1 current=cand else keep best
```

Weights: Need avoid all zero. `make_weights(c, style, state)`:
```
def make_weights(c, mode, beta, frac, power):
  L=len(c); M=c.max(); if M<=0: return np.ones(L)/L
  if mode==0: # quant
     K=max(1,min(L,int(frac*L)))
     if K>=L: thr=0
     else:
       thr=np.partition(c, L-K)[L-K]
       w=np.maximum(c-thr,0.0)
       sw=w.sum()
       if sw<=0:
          idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]; w=np.zeros(L); w[idx]=1.0
       else: w/=sw
     if power !=1: w = w**power? But if w normalized, power changes sum; renormalize.
  elif mode==1: # softmax
     z=beta*(c-M); w=np.exp(z); w+=1e-12; w/=w.sum()
  return w
```
For quant, if we apply power>1, threshold top less. We'll keep power=1. Adapt frac.

`local_optimize` detailed code:
```
def local_optimize(a, budget_s, seed=0, sym=False, mode=0, gamma=0.1, beta=1.0, frac=0.3, use_lp=False):
   t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+max(0,budget_s)
   n=len(a); target=float(n)
   a=normalize_sequence(a,target)
   if sym: a=sym(a)
   c=fast_conv(a); s=float(a.sum()); score=...
   best=a.copy(); best_score=score; current=a.copy(); cur_score=score
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   ymom=np.zeros(n)
   temp=0.005
   gamma=...; eta=0.3; beta=...; frac=...
   stagnant=0; it=0; lp_done=0; lp_time_left=...
   while time.monotonic()<deadline:
      it+=1
      # If current invalid reset
      if not finite or sum<0.01: current=best; c=fast_conv(current); cur_score=best_score; ymom*=0.3
      # weights
      if mode==0: w=make_weights(c,0,beta,frac,1)
      else: w=make_weights(c,1,beta,frac,1)
      D=cross_corr_with_weights(current,w)
      Dmean=float(D.mean())
      if Dmean<=1e-12: Dmean=1.0
      if mode==2 exp:
         g=D-Dmean; scale=max(abs(g)); if scale< tiny break; g/=scale
         logf=-eta*g
      else:
         rel=D/Dmean
         logf=-gamma*np.log(np.maximum(rel,1e-8))
      if sym: logf=0.5*(logf+logf[::-1])
      ymom=0.6*ymom+0.4*logf
      step=np.clip(ymom, -5,5)
      cand=current*np.exp(step)
      cand=normalize_sequence(cand,target)
      if sym: cand=sym(cand); cand=normalize_sequence(cand,target)
      c2=fast_conv(cand); s2=...; new_score=...
      if finite:
         if new_score < best_score*(1-1e-12): best=cand.copy(); best_score=new_score; stagnant=0
         if new_score <= cur_score*(1+temp)+1e-12:
            current=cand; cur_score=new_score; c=c2
            temp*=0.997
            # adapt gamma
            if new_score < cur_score_old? track old? store old_score before accept
            stagnant+=1
         else:
            gamma*=0.85; beta=max(0.2,beta*0.8); ymom*=0.5; stagnant+=1
      else:
         gamma*=0.8; current=best; cur_score=best_score; c=fast_conv(current); stagnant+=1
      if it%100==0:
         frac = max(0.1, min(0.6, 0.6 - 0.5*(best_score-1.0?)))? maybe based on score.
         beta = min(20.0, beta + 0.2)
      if stagnant>300:
         # perturb
         pert=make_perturbation(current, rng, sym)
         pc=fast_conv(pert); psc=...
         if psc < best_score*1.05:
             current=pert; cur_score=psc; c=pc
         else:
             current=best.copy(); cur_score=best_score; c=fast_conv(current)
         temp=min(0.05,temp*1.5)
         gamma=max(0.02,gamma*0.8)
         ymom=np.zeros(n)
         stagnant=0
      if use_lp and n<=1024 and lp_done<1 and deadline-time.monotonic()>60:
         g=lp_improve(current, time_limit_s=30.0)
         lp_done+=1
         if g is not None:
            cg=fast_conv(g); sg=...; if finite:
               # Maybe blend: try blends 1.0 g, 0.7g+0.3current, 0.4 etc
               for t in (1.0,0.7,0.4):
                  cand = t*g + (1-t)*current; normalize, sym? score; choose best < current
               accept best improving
      # Time check each loop at top.
   return best,best_score
```
Need track old_score for adaptation. Use.

Exp mode logf may cause all positive? Centering after ymom? We'll subtract mean of ymom before exp? In exp mode, g centered, mean0. In inverse mode, logf not centered but normalization handles. For safety, `ymom = ymom - np.mean(ymom)` before step; preserves scale.

def make_perturbation(a,rng,sym=False):
   a=a.copy(); n=len(a)
   if n>3:
      t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
      z=np.zeros(n)
      for m in range(1,6):
         amp=0.4/(m+0.5)
         ph=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
         z += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*t+ph)
      a *= np.exp(z)
   # random blocks
   nb=int(rng.integers(2,6)); edges... values...
   mix = 0.5*a +0.5*block; normalize; if sym sym.
```

`optimize_length`:
```
def optimize_length(n,budget,parent,seed,random_count=60,use_lp=False, sym=False, mode=0):
  t0=..., deadline=t0+budget
  if budget<5: return normalized parent/ones
  starts=make_starts(n,rng,parent,random_count)
  # Limit starts based on budget
  k=max(1,min(len(starts), int(budget/30)+1, 6))
  best_seq=None; best_score=inf
  for i in range(k):
     if time>=deadline-10 break
     sc,seq=starts[i] if sc<best_score update
     time_each=(deadline-time.monotonic())/ (k-i)
     mode_choice: alternate 0 and 1, sym sometimes.
     seq2,sc2=local_optimize(seq, min(time_each, deadline-time.monotonic()-5), seed+i, sym=(rng<0.3), mode=mode_choice, use_lp=use_lp and n<=768)
     if finite update
  # Additionally optimize best with remaining time
  if best_seq is not None and remaining>20:
     seq3,sc3=local_optimize(best_seq, remaining, seed+999, sym=False, mode=1-beta maybe, use_lp=...)
     update
  return best
```
But `local_optimize` for top starts with small time maybe not much. We can also run a "global" optimizer on best for 50% time. The above: allocate top k each equal, then final on best. Good.

In `propose_candidate`, schedule:

# small warm-up

Need maintain `global_best` exact? Use fast score. Parent for next level should be best sequence so far (maybe 512 or 1024). If search at 512 finds 1.51, upsample to 1024 and 2048. Good.

Should we include 4096? Score lower bound slightly lower, but local optimizer n=4096 N=16384/8192; iterations maybe slower. If time abundant, could final_n=4096 with upsample of 2048. But local_optimize at 2048 with inverse update: cross_corr N=8192, conv N=4096; per iteration maybe 0.01-0.03s. 1100s ~ 30k-100k iter? Actually 0.03 ->36k. Enough.

Potential issue: `fast_conv` with FFT N=4096 per iter, `cross_corr` N=8194? Next pow2 for n=2048: 3n-2=6142 ->8192. Two FFTs: conv rfft(a,N=4096), irfft; cross rfft(rev a,N=8192), rfft(w,N=8192), irfft. ~3 FFTs plus irffts. 36k* maybe 5ms =180s? Actually okay. Python overhead maybe high but fine.

Can we reduce cross N by using circular shift trick for valid convolution? Cross-corr length 3n-2 requires N≥3n-2. But we only need first half of correlation (i=0..n-1) of w length 2n-1 with a. Since w and a supports lengths, the linear correlation segment length n could be computed via circular convolution with N=2n maybe? Let's check. We need r_i=∑_{j=0}^{n-1} a_j w_{i+j}, i=0..n-1. This is like sliding dot product. If we circularly convolve reversed a (length n) with w (length 2n-1) with N power>=? circular aliasing: For N=2n, conv indices wrap. We need linear values at t=i+n-1 (range n-1..2n-2). If N=2n (>=? circular length 2n), linear conv length 3n-2 >2n, so values in range t=n-1..2n-2 may be aliased from t-N = -n+1..-2? def next_pow2(m):
    m=int(max(1,m))
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()

Normalize:
```
def normalize_sequence(a, target_sum):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size==0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,None)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s<1e-15:
        a.fill(max(1e-12, target_sum/float(a.size)))
        return a
    a=(float(target_sum)/s)*a
    # cap at 1000
    cap=1000.0
    for _ in range(4):
        a=np.clip(a,0.0,cap)
        s=float(a.sum())
        if s<1e-15:
            a.fill(max(1e-12, target_sum/float(a.size), 1e-12/cap?))
            break
        a=(target_sum/s)*a
        if float(a.max())<=cap:
            break
    if float(a.sum())<0.01:
        a.fill(float(target_sum)/a.size)
    return a
```
If target_sum/size > cap (n>1000? target_sum=n =>1; okay). For target_sum maybe float len. Good.

But if after clipping sum < target, rescale up to target may exceed cap; loop converges? For n=5, target5, if one value >1000, clip to1000 sum maybe >target? If sum>target, scaling down no exceed. If sum<target, scaling up may exceed cap on same value, clip again. In 4 iterations likely. Final max may remain >cap if impossible? But target/size=1, cap large. No issue.

`fast_conv`:
```
def fast_conv(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.empty(0)
    L=2*n-1
    N=next_pow2(L)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:L]
    c=np.asarray(c,dtype=np.float64)
    c=np.where(c>0.0,c,0.0) # remove small negative
    return c
```
But `np.fft.irfft` may produce tiny negatives; clip.

`fast_score`:
```
def fast_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return inf
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s<0.01: return inf
    c=fast_conv(a)
    if c.size==0 or not np.isfinite(c).all(): return inf
    maxc=float(np.max(c))
    return float(2.0*n*maxc/(s*s))
```
We don't prepare sum target; scale invariant. For state, normalized sum n. Good.

`cross_corr_weights`:
```
def cross_corr_weights(a,w):
    n=len(a); if n==0 return np.empty
    L=2*n-1
    w=np.asarray(w,dtype=np.float64)
    if w.size!=L: w=w[:L]; if shorter pad?
    N=next_pow2(2*n)
    ar=a[::-1].copy()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(ar,N)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw,N)
    # segment t=n-1 to 2n-2
    seg=conv[n-1:n-1+n]
    seg=np.where(np.isfinite(seg),seg,0.0)
    return seg
```
Need ensure w length L. `np.fft.rfft` length N, if w length <N ok; if length >? w size L <=N? For n=1, L=1,N=2 ok. For n=2,L=3,N=4. Good. If w longer? no.

Actually cross_corr formula with N=2n: desired segment conv[n-1 : n-1+n]. For n=3,N=8: ar conv w linear length 9? Wait a length3, w length5 -> linear length7, max index6. N=8. Segment indices2..4 length3. good. For n power2 N=2n linear length3n-2; max index3n-3; for n=3 max6, N8 no alias for t2? t+N=10>6. good.

Weights function:
```
def make_weights(c, mode, beta, frac):
    c=np.asarray(c)
    L=c.size
    if L==0: return np.empty(0)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m<=0:
        return np.ones(L)/L
    if mode >= 10: # quant
       K=int(max(1, min(L, round(L*max(0.02,min(0.95,frac))))))
       if K>=L:
          w=c.copy(); w=np.maximum(w,0)/...
       else:
          thr=np.partition(c, L-K)[L-K]
          w=np.maximum(c-thr,0.0)
          sw=float(w.sum())
          if sw<=0:
             idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
             w=np.zeros(L); w[idx]=1.0
          else: w/=sw
       return w
    else: # softmax
       z=beta*(c-m)
       # clip z to avoid overflow; min maybe -50
       z=np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
       w=np.exp(z)
       sw=float(w.sum())
       if sw<=0: w[idx]=1? 
       else w/=sw
       return w
```
Softmax with z≤0, all >=e^-50. Good. For beta=0? If beta=0 w uniform.

def make_perturbation(a, rng, sym=False, scale=0.35):
    n=len(a); out=a.copy()
    if n>4:
       t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
       z=np.zeros(n)
       for m in range(1,6):
          amp=scale/(0.5+m)
          ph=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
          z += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*t+ph)
       out = out*np.exp(z)
       if rng.random()<0.4:
          nb=int(rng.integers(2,7)); edges=...
       else...
    out=normalize_sequence(out,float(n))
    if sym: out=sym(out); out=normalize_sequence(out,float(n))
    return out
```

def make_block_profile(n,rng,sym=False):
   nb=int(rng.integers(2,min(20,n//2+1)))
   edges=np.linspace(0,n,nb+1).astype(int); ensure unique
   vals=np.abs(rng.normal(0.5,1.0,nb))+0.05
   # occasionally zero block
   if nb>2 and rng.random()<0.3: vals[int(rng.integers(0,nb))]=0.0
   a=np.repeat(vals,np.diff(edges))
   if sym: a=0.5*(a+a[::-1])
   return normalize_sequence(a,float(n))
```

def make_endpoint_profile(n, gamma, side, base=0.05):
  idx=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=float)
  left=idx**(-gamma)
  right=idx[::-1]**(-gamma)
  if side==0: a=left
  elif side==1: a=right
  else: a=left+right
  a=base+a
  return a
```

```
def upsample_sequence(seq,n):
  seq=np.asarray(seq,float); m=seq.size
  if m==0: return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n),float(n))
  if m==1: return normalize_sequence(np.full(n,seq[0]),float(n))
  xold=np.linspace(0,1,m); xnew=np.linspace(0,1,n)
  out=np.interp(xnew,xold,seq)
  return normalize_sequence(out,float(n))
```

def get_parent_sequence():
  try:
    p=globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    if p is not None and isinstance(p,(list,tuple,np.ndarray)) and len(p)>0:
       a=np.asarray(p,float)
       a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0)
       a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
       if a.sum()>0.01:
          return normalize_sequence(a,float(len(a)))
  except: pass
  return None

def local_optimize(a,budget_s,seed=0,sym=False,update_style=0,gamma=0.10,beta=1.0,frac=0.35,use_lp=False):
    budget_s=float(max(0,budget_s))
    if budget_s <=0: return normalize_sequence(a,float(len(a))), fast_score(...)
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+budget_s
    n=len(a); target=float(n)
    a=normalize_sequence(a,target)
    if sym: a=sym(a); a=normalize_sequence(a,target)
    c=fast_conv(a); s=sum; score=...
    if not finite: return uniform...
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score; current=a.copy(); cur_score=score; cur_c=c
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    ymom=np.zeros(n)
    temp=0.004
    stagnant=0; it=0; lp_done=0
    gamma_cur=gamma; beta_cur=beta; frac_cur=frac
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       it +=1
       # reset if invalid
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(current)) or float(current.sum())<0.01:
          current=best.copy(); cur_c=fast_conv(current); cur_score=best_score; ymom*=0.5
       # target hyperparams
       if best_score > 1.75:
          frac_target=0.6
          beta_target=6.0
       elif best_score > 1.55:
          frac_target=0.4
          beta_target=12.0
       else:
          frac_target=0.2
          beta_target=25.0
       frac_cur +=0.05*(frac_target-frac_cur)
       beta_cur +=0.05*(beta_target-beta_cur)
       if update_style==0:
          w=make_weights(cur_c,1,beta_cur,frac_cur) # use softmax or quant? maybe quant? Let's use quant for inverse? 
       else:
          w=make_weights(cur_c,1,beta_cur,frac_cur)
```

Let's implement `add_candidate` top-level:
```
def add_candidate(pool,seq,score):
    if seq is None: return
    try: sc=float(score)
    except: return
    if not np.isfinite(sc): return
    arr=np.asarray(seq,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if arr.size==0: return
    pool.append((sc,arr))
    pool.sort(key=_cand_score)
    if len(pool)>20: pool.pop()
```

def optimize_length(n,budget,parent,seed=0,random_count=60,use_lp=False):
   deadline=...
   if budget<6:
      if parent is not None: seq=upsample(parent,n)
      else seq=np.ones(n)
      return seq,fast_score(seq)
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   starts=make_starts(n,rng,parent,random_count)
   if not starts: seq=ones...
   # choose k
   max_starts = max(1, min(len(starts), int(budget/25)+1, 6))
   best_seq=None; best_score=inf
   for i in range(max_starts):
       if time>=deadline-5: break
       sc,seq=starts[i]
       if sc<best_score: best_seq=seq.copy(); best_score=sc
       rem=(deadline-time.monotonic())/float(max_starts-i)
       time_this=max(2.0, min(rem, deadline-time.monotonic()-2.0))
       # alternate update styles
       style = 0 if (i%2==0) else 1
       sym = (rng.random()<0.25)
       seq2,sc2=local_optimize(seq,time_this,seed=int(seed*100)+i,sym=sym,update_style=style,use_lp=use_lp)
       if seq2 is not None and finite and sc2<best_score:
          best_seq=seq2; best_score=sc2
   # final deep on best
   rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
   if best_seq is not None and rem>20:
       # Use style not used? run inverse if best from exp?
       style=0 if best? maybe 1
       seq3,sc3=local_optimize(best_seq, rem, seed=int(seed+777), sym=False, update_style=1, use_lp=use_lp and rem>120)
       update
   if best_seq is None: fallback
   return best_seq,best_score
```

Let's write `lp_improve`:
```
def lp_improve(a, time_limit_s=30.0):
    try:
       from scipy.optimize import linprog
    except Exception:
       return None
    a=np.asarray(a,float); n=a.size
    if n<2 or n>1024: return None
    c=fast_conv(a); m=float(np.max(c)); if not finite or m<=0: return None
    M=m*1.0005
    try:
       A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
       for j in range(n):
          A[j:j+n,j]=a
       cb_ub=np.full(2*n-1, M, dtype=np.float64)
       obj=-np.ones(n,dtype=np.float64)
       bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n
       opts={'time_limit': float(time_limit_s), 'primal_feasibility_tolerance':1e-7, 'dual_feasibility_tolerance':1e-7}
       res=linprog(obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=cb_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options=opts)
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res is None or getattr(res,'success',False) is False:
        # If status 3 time limit maybe still x
        if res is None or getattr(res,'x',None) is None: return None
    g=np.asarray(res.x,float)
    g=np.where(np.isfinite(g),g,0.0); g=np.clip(g,0.0,1000.0)
    if g.sum()<=0: return None
    g=normalize_sequence(g,float(n))
    return g
```
HiGHS options `time_limit` may not accepted if presolve? It should. If not, exception maybe? The options dict passed only if chosen; if unknown option, HiGHS may error? Scipy linprog options are method-specific and ignored? It may set. To be safe, don't include time_limit? But then solver could run long. We can pass `time_limit`? In scipy HiGHS options, parameter is `time_limit`? I think yes for `options={'time_limit': ...}`. If not, may raise ValueError? Could wrap in second try without options:
```
try: res=linprog(..., options=opts)
except TypeError: res=linprog(..., options={})
```
But we need time limit. Could not use time limit and rely on overall propose deadline? It might exceed. Use `time_limit_s` within options. If option invalid, catch all and maybe no LP.

Maybe use `method='highs-ds'`? default. Fine.

</think>

```python
"""
Searches for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a))**2.

Key ideas:
- The objective is reduced to making the coefficients of the square of a
  non-negative polynomial as flat as possible.
- Use FFT-based convolution for fast objective evaluation.
- Use an inverse multiplicative update driven by the subgradient from the
  currently overloaded convolution coefficients.  If D_i is the responsibility
  of coefficient i for overloaded bins, update a_i by (mean(D)/D_i)^gamma.
- Generate very diverse starts, including one-sided/two-sided endpoint power-law
  profiles, which are important for near-optimal solutions.
- Occasionally use an LP jump: given the current sequence a and current bound M,
  solve max(sum g) s.t. (a * g)_k <= M, g >= 0, and move toward the LP solution.
- For rougher lengths, refine and upsample to larger lengths.
- Finally validate the best candidates with the provided exact evaluator.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _cand_score(cand):
    return cand[0]


def _pair_score(pair):
    return pair[0]


def next_pow2(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_sequence(a, target_sum):
    """Clip to nonnegative, normalize to target sum, keep values <= 1000."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(max(1, int(float(target_sum))), dtype=np.float64)

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)

    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a.fill(max(1e-12, float(target_sum) / float(a.size)))
        return a

    scale = float(target_sum) / s
    a *= scale

    cap = 1000.0
    for _ in range(6):
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, cap)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if s <= 1e-15:
            a.fill(max(1e-12, float(target_sum) / float(a.size)))
            break
        a *= float(target_sum) / s
        if float(a.max()) <= cap:
            break

    # If impossible to reach the exact target because of the 1000 cap, add a
    # small uniform mass before final clipping.
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < float(target_sum) * 0.98:
        deficit = float(target_sum) - s
        if deficit > 0.0 and a.size > 0:
            a += deficit / float(a.size)
            a = np.clip(a, 0.0, cap)
            s = float(a.sum())
            if s > 1e-15:
                a *= float(target_sum) / s
                a = np.clip(a, 0.0, cap)

    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        a.fill(float(target_sum) / float(a.size))
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, cap)

    return a


def fast_conv(a):
    """FFT linear convolution a * a."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = next_pow2(L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:L]
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c = np.clip(c, 0.0, None)
    return c


def fast_score(a):
    """Fast analogue of evaluate_sequence."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return float("inf")

    c = fast_conv(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")

    maxc = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(maxc):
        return float("inf")

    return float(2.0 * n * maxc / (s * s))


def score_array_c(a, c):
    n = len(a)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if n == 0 or c.size == 0 or s < 0.01:
        return float("inf")
    maxc = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(maxc):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * float(n) * maxc / (s * s))


def cross_corr_weights(a, w):
    """
    Returns D_i = sum_j a_j * w_{i+j}, where w is the weight vector over
    convolution coefficients (length 2n-1).  This is the gradient (up to a
    factor of 2) for the weighted sum of convolution coefficients.
    """
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    L = 2 * n - 1
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    if w.size != L:
        tmp = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(L, w.size)
        tmp[:m] = w[:m]
        w = tmp

    N = next_pow2(2 * n)
    ar = a[::-1].copy()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(ar, N)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fa * fw, N)

    seg = conv[n - 1:n - 1 + n]
    seg = np.asarray(seg, dtype=np.float64)
    seg = np.where(np.isfinite(seg), seg, 0.0)
    seg = np.clip(seg, 0.0, None)
    return seg


def sym(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    return 0.5 * (a + a[::-1])


def upsample_sequence(seq, n):
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    m = seq.size
    n = int(n)
    if m == 0:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), float(n))
    if m == 1:
        return normalize_sequence(np.full(n, float(seq[0]), dtype=np.float64), float(n))
    if m == n:
        return normalize_sequence(seq, float(n))

    xold = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    xnew = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out = np.interp(xnew, xold, seq)
    return normalize_sequence(out, float(n))


def get_parent_sequence():
    try:
        p = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if p is not None and len(p) > 0:
            a = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
            a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
            a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if float(a.sum()) > 0.01:
                return normalize_sequence(a, float(len(a)))
    except Exception:
        pass
    return None


def make_block_profile(n, rng, symm=False):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64), 1.0)

    max_blocks = max(2, min(24, n // 2 + 1))
    nb = int(rng.integers(2, max_blocks + 1))
    edges = np.linspace(0, n, nb + 1).astype(int)
    edges[0] = 0
    edges[-1] = n
    lengths = np.diff(edges)
    if np.any(lengths < 0):
        edges = np.array([i * n // nb for i in range(nb + 1)], dtype=int)
        edges[-1] = n
        lengths = np.diff(edges)

    vals = np.abs(rng.normal(0.6, 1.0, nb)) + 0.05
    if nb > 2 and rng.random() < 0.25:
        vals[int(rng.integers(0, nb))] = 0.0

    a = np.repeat(vals, lengths)
    if a.size != n:
        a = np.interp(np.linspace(0, 1, n), np.linspace(0, 1, a.size), a)

    if symm:
        a = sym(a)
    return normalize_sequence(a, float(n))


def make_smooth_profile(n, rng, symm=False):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), float(n))

    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    d = int(rng.integers(3, 8))
    z = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for m in range(1, d + 1):
        amp = rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0) / (0.5 + m)
        ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
        if rng.random() < 0.55:
            z += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * t + ph)
        else:
            z += amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * t + ph)

    if float(np.max(z)) > 0:
        z -= float(np.max(z))
    a = np.exp(np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0))
    if symm:
        a = sym(a)
    return normalize_sequence(a, float(n))


def make_perturbation(a, rng, symm=False, strength=0.45):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    if n > 5:
        t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        z = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for m in range(1, 6):
            amp = strength / (0.5 + m)
            ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
            z += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * t + ph)
        a *= np.exp(np.clip(z, -4.0, 4.0))

    if rng.random() < 0.45:
        b = make_block_profile(n, rng, symm=False)
        a = 0.65 * a + 0.35 * b

    if symm:
        a = sym(a)
    return normalize_sequence(a, float(n))


def try_add_start(starts, a, n):
    try:
        arr = normalize_sequence(np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64), float(n))
        if arr.size != n:
            return
        sc = fast_score(arr)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            starts.append((float(sc), arr.copy()))
    except Exception:
        pass


def make_starts(n, rng, parent, random_count=60):
    n = int(n)
    starts = []
    if n <= 0:
        return [(float("inf"), None)]

    try_add_start(starts, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), n)

    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    x = idx / max(1, n - 1)
    t = (idx + 0.5) / float(n)
    u = 2.0 * t - 1.0

    # Ramps and exponentials.
    for s in (-3.0, -1.5, -0.75, -0.25, 0.0, 0.25, 0.75, 1.5, 3.0):
        try_add_start(starts, 1.0 + s * x, n)
        try_add_start(starts, np.exp(s * x), n)
        try_add_start(starts, np.exp(-s * x), n)

    # Simple polynomial / cosine shapes.
    for alpha in (-2.0, -1.0, -0.5, -0.2, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        for p in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
            try_add_start(starts, 1.0 + alpha * np.abs(u) ** p, n)
            try_add_start(starts, 1.0 + alpha * (t - 0.5) ** p, n)
        try_add_start(starts, 1.0 + alpha * np.cos(np.pi * u), n)
        try_add_start(starts, 1.0 + alpha * np.sin(np.pi * t), n)

    # Endpoint power-law profiles.  These are extremely important.
    pos = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    rev = pos[::-1]
    gammas = (0.25, 0.35, 0.45, 0.50, 0.55, 0.65, 0.75)

    left_by_g = {}
    right_by_g = {}
    for g in gammas:
        try_add_start(starts, 0.05 + pos ** (-g), n)
        try_add_start(starts, 0.05 + rev ** (-g), n)
        left_by_g[g] = pos ** (-g)
        right_by_g[g] = rev ** (-g)

    ratios = (0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 5.0)
    for gl in gammas:
        left = left_by_g[gl]
        for gr in gammas:
            right = right_by_g[gr]
            for w in ratios:
                try_add_start(starts, 0.02 + w * left + right, n)
                try_add_start(starts, 0.02 + left + w * right, n)
            try_add_start(starts, 0.02 + left + right, n)
            try_add_start(starts, 0.02 + left - 0.25 * right, n)
            try_add_start(starts, 0.02 + right - 0.25 * left, n)

    # Beta-like power profiles, including one-sided endpoint singularities.
    tt = np.maximum(t, 1e-8)
    rt = np.maximum(1.0 - t, 1e-8)
    for p in (-0.75, -0.50, -0.25, 0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        try_add_start(starts, tt ** p + 1e-6, n)
        try_add_start(starts, rt ** p + 1e-6, n)
        if p < 0:
            try_add_start(starts, tt ** p + rt ** (0.5 * p) + 1e-6, n)
            try_add_start(starts, tt ** (0.5 * p) + rt ** p + 1e-6, n)
        try_add_start(starts, tt ** p + rt ** (-p) + 1e-6, n)

    # Random profiles.
    for _ in range(max(0, int(random_count))):
        kind = int(rng.integers(0, 7))
        if kind < 2:
            a = make_smooth_profile(n, rng, symm=False)
        elif kind == 2:
            a = make_block_profile(n, rng, symm=False)
        elif kind == 3:
            p = rng.uniform(-0.75, 0.75)
            q = rng.uniform(-0.75, 0.75)
            a = tt ** p + rt ** q + 1e-6
        elif kind == 4:
            g = rng.uniform(0.35, 0.75)
            mix = rng.uniform(0.05, 3.0)
            a = 0.02 + mix * pos ** (-g) + rev ** (-g)
        elif kind == 5:
            alpha = rng.uniform(-2.0, 2.0)
            p = rng.uniform(0.4, 2.6)
            a = 1.0 + alpha * np.abs(u) ** p
        else:
            a = make_block_profile(n, rng, symm=True)
        try_add_start(starts, a, n)

        if rng.random() < 0.25:
            try_add_start(starts, sym(a), n)

    if parent is not None and len(parent) > 0:
        try_add_start(starts, upsample_sequence(parent, n), n)

    if not starts:
        starts.append((2.0, normalize_sequence(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), float(n))))

    starts.sort(key=_pair_score)
    return starts


def make_weights(c, use_softmax, beta, frac):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    L = c.size
    if L == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)

    if use_softmax:
        z = beta * (c - m)
        z = np.clip(z, -60.0, 0.0)
        w = np.exp(z)
        sw = float(w.sum())
        if sw <= 0.0:
            idx = int(np.argmax(c))
            w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
            w[idx] = 1.0
        else:
            w /= sw
        return w

    frac = float(max(0.02, min(0.95, frac)))
    K = int(max(1, min(L, int(round(L * frac)))))
    if K >= L:
        w = c.copy()
        sw = float(w.sum())
        if sw <= 0.0:
            w = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64)
            sw = float(L)
        w /= sw
        return w

    thr = float(np.partition(c, L - K)[L - K])
    w = np.maximum(c - thr, 0.0)
    sw = float(w.sum())
    if sw <= 0.0:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[idx] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= sw
    return w


def local_optimize(a, budget_s, seed=0, symm=False, update_style=0,
                   gamma=0.10, eta=0.30, beta=1.0, frac=0.35, use_lp=False):
    """
    inverse/update_style=0: quantized overloaded-coefficient weights + inverse
                           multiplicative balancing.
    exp/update_style=1: softmax weights + centered multiplicative gradient step.
    """
    budget_s = float(max(0.0, budget_s))
    n = int(len(a))
    if n == 0:
        a = normalize_sequence(np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64), 512.0)
        n = a.size
    target = float(n)

    try:
        a = normalize_sequence(np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64), target)
    except Exception:
        a = normalize_sequence(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), target)

    if symm:
        a = sym(a)
        a = normalize_sequence(a, target)

    c = fast_conv(a)
    score = score_array_c(a, c)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        a = normalize_sequence(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), target)
        c = fast_conv(a)
        score = score_array_c(a, c)
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            return a, float("inf")

    best = a.copy()
    best_score = score
    current = a.copy()
    current_score = score
    current_c = c

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    ymom = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    gamma_cur = float(gamma)
    eta_cur = float(eta)
    beta_cur = float(beta)
    frac_cur = float(frac)
    temp = 0.0045
    stagnant = 0
    it = 0
    lp_done = 0

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + budget_s
    if budget_s <= 0.0:
        return best, best_score

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1

        if (not np.all(np.isfinite(current))) or float(current.sum()) < 0.01:
            current = best.copy()
            current_c = fast_conv(current)
            current_score = best_score
            ymom *= 0.35

        # Keep focusing narrower as the score approaches the target region.
        if best_score > 1.75:
            frac_target, beta_target = 0.60, 6.0
        elif best_score > 1.58:
            frac_target, beta_target = 0.40, 12.0
        elif best_score > 1.51:
            frac_target, beta_target = 0.25, 20.0
        else:
            frac_target, beta_target = 0.15, 30.0

        frac_cur += 0.06 * (frac_target - frac_cur)
        beta_cur += 0.06 * (beta_target - beta_cur)

        if update_style == 1:
            w = make_weights(current_c, True, beta_cur, frac_cur)
        else:
            w = make_weights(current_c, False, beta_cur, frac_cur)

        D = cross_corr_weights(current, w)
        Dmean = float(D.mean())
        if Dmean <= 1e-12:
            Dmean = 1.0

        if update_style == 1:
            g = D - Dmean
            gs = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
            if gs < 1e-12:
                stagnant += 100
                if stagnant > 500:
                    current = best.copy()
                    current_c = fast_conv(current)
                    current_score = best_score
                    ymom *= 0.25
                    stagnant = 0
                continue
            g /= gs
            logf = -eta_cur * g
        else:
            rel = D / Dmean
            rel = np.clip(rel, 1e-7, None)
            logf = -gamma_cur * np.log(rel)

        if symm:
            logf = sym(logf)

        logf = np.asarray(logf, dtype=np.float64)
        logf = np.where(np.isfinite(logf), logf, 0.0)
        logf -= float(np.mean(logf))
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(logf)))
        if scale < 1e-12:
            stagnant += 1
            if stagnant > 800:
                current = best.copy()
                current_c = fast_conv(current)
                current_score = best_score
                ymom *= 0.25
                stagnant = 0
            continue

        logf /= scale
        ymom = 0.62 * ymom + 0.38 * logf
        step = np.clip(ymom, -4.0, 4.0)

        cand = current * np.exp(step)
        cand = normalize_sequence(cand, target)
        if symm:
            cand = sym(cand)
            cand = normalize_sequence(cand, target)

        cand_c = fast_conv(cand)
        cand_score = score_array_c(cand, cand_c)
        old_current = current_score

        if np.isfinite(cand_score):
            improved_best = cand_score < best_score * (1.0 - 1e-12)
            if improved_best:
                best = cand.copy()
                best_score = cand_score
                stagnant = 0

            if cand_score <= current_score * (1.0 + temp) + 1e-12:
                current = cand
                current_c = cand_c
                current_score = cand_score
                temp = max(1e-5, temp * 0.9972)

                if cand_score < old_current:
                    gamma_cur = min(0.55, gamma_cur * 1.04)
                    eta_cur = min(1.0, eta_cur * 1.04)
                    beta_cur = min(60.0, beta_cur * 1.07)
                else:
                    gamma_cur = max(0.025, gamma_cur * 0.985)
                    eta_cur = max(0.05, eta_cur * 0.985)

                stagnant += 1
            else:
                gamma_cur = max(0.025, gamma_cur * 0.85)
                eta_cur = max(0.05, eta_cur * 0.85)
                beta_cur = max(0.1, beta_cur * 0.9)
                ymom *= 0.45
                stagnant += 1
        else:
            gamma_cur = max(0.025, gamma_cur * 0.8)
            eta_cur = max(0.05, eta_cur * 0.8)
            current = best.copy()
            current_c = fast_conv(current)
            current_score = best_score
            ymom *= 0.25
            stagnant += 1

        if stagnant > 350:
            pert = make_perturbation(current, rng, symm, 0.50)
            pc = fast_conv(pert)
            ps = score_array_c(pert, pc)
            if np.isfinite(ps) and ps < max(best_score * 1.06, current_score * 1.30):
                current = pert
                current_c = pc
                current_score = ps
            else:
                current = best.copy()
                current_c = fast_conv(current)
                current_score = best_score
            temp = min(0.06, temp * 1.6)
            gamma_cur = max(0.03, gamma_cur * 0.75)
            eta_cur = max(0.08, eta_cur * 0.75)
            ymom = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            stagnant = 0

        if use_lp and n <= 768 and lp_done < 1 and (deadline - time.monotonic()) > 75.0:
            g = lp_improve(current, 25.0)
            lp_done += 1
            if g is not None:
                blend_seq = current
                blend_score = current_score
                for al in (1.0, 0.8, 0.5, 0.2):
                    bl = al * g + (1.0 - al) * current
                    bl = normalize_sequence(bl, target)
                    if symm:
                        bl = sym(bl)
                        bl = normalize_sequence(bl, target)
                    bc = fast_conv(bl)
                    bs = score_array_c(bl, bc)
                    if np.isfinite(bs) and bs < blend_score:
                        blend_score = bs
                        blend_seq = bl

                if np.isfinite(blend_score) and blend_score < solo + 1e-12:
                    current = blend_seq
                    current_c = fast_conv(current)
                    current_score = blend_score

                if np.isfinite(blend_score) and blend_score < solo:
                    best = blend_seq.copy()
                    best_score = blend_score

    return best, best_score


def lp_improve(a, time_limit_s=25.0):
    """
    Given a current sequence a, let M=max(a*a).  Solve LP
        maximize sum g
        subject to (a*g)_k <= M,
                   g >= 0,
    and return the normalized LP solution.
    """
    try:
        from scipy.optimize import linprog
    except Exception:
        return None

    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n < 2 or n > 1024:
        return None
    if time_limit_s <= 0.0:
        return None

    c = fast_conv(a)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        return None

    M = m * 1.0005
    try:
        A = np.zeros((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
        for j in range(n):
            A[j:j + n, j] = a

        b_ub = np.full(2 * n - 1, M, dtype=np.float64)
        c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
        options = {
            "time_limit": float(max(1.0, time_limit_s)),
            "primal_feasibility_tolerance": 1e-7,
            "dual_feasibility_tolerance": 1e-7,
        }

        res = linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options=options,
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is None:
        return None

    g = getattr(res, "x", None)
    if g is None:
        return None

    g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    g = np.clip(g, 0.0, 1e6)
    if float(g.sum()) <= 0.0:
        return None

    return normalize_sequence(g, float(n))


def optimize_length(n, budget_s, parent, seed=0, random_count=70, use_lp=False):
    n = int(n)
    budget_s = float(max(0.0, budget_s))
    if n <= 0:
        n = 512

    if parent is None:
        parent = normalize_sequence(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), float(n))

    if budget_s < 6.0:
        seq = upsample_sequence(parent, n)
        return seq, fast_score(seq)

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + budget_s
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    starts = make_starts(n, rng, parent, random_count)
    if not starts:
        seq = normalize_sequence(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), float(n))
        return seq, fast_score(seq)

    max_k = max(1, min(len(starts), int(budget_s / 22.0) + 1, 6))
    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    for i in range(max_k):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 3.0:
            break
        sc, seq = starts[i]
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_seq = seq.copy()
            best_score = sc

        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        time_each = rem / float(max_k - i)
        time_this = max(2.0, min(time_each, rem - 1.0))
        if time_this <= 0.0:
            break

        style = 0 if (i % 2 == 0) else 1
        symm = bool(rng.random() < 0.22)
        try:
            seq2, sc2 = local_optimize(
                seq,
                time_this,
                seed=int(seed * 1000 + 17 + i),
                symm=symm,
                update_style=style,
                use_lp=False,
            )
        except Exception:
            seq2, sc2 = None, float("inf")

        if seq2 is not None and np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score:
            best_seq = seq2
            best_score = sc2

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = normalize_sequence(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), float(n))
        best_score = fast_score(best_seq)

    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 12.0:
        # Final deep refinement on the best sequence for this length.
        try:
            seq3, sc3 = local_optimize(
                best_seq,
                rem,
                seed=int(seed * 1000 + 911),
                symm=False,
                update_style=1,
                gamma=0.08,
                beta=4.0,
                frac=0.35,
                use_lp=use_lp,
            )
        except Exception:
            seq3, sc3 = None, float("inf")

        if seq3 is not None and np.isfinite(sc3) and sc3 < best_score:
            best_seq = seq3
            best_score = sc3

    return best_seq, best_score


def add_candidate(pool, seq, score):
    if seq is None:
        return
    try:
        sc = float(score)
    except Exception:
        return
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return
    arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return
    pool.append((sc, arr))
    pool.sort(key=_cand_score)
    if len(pool) > 24:
        pool.pop()


def sanitize_for_return(seq):
    """
    Convert to a list of Python floats.  Avoid values above 1000 by scaling
    the whole sequence down, which preserves the scale-invariant objective.
    """
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if seq.size == 0:
        return [1.0] * 512

    seq = np.where(np.isfinite(seq), seq, 0.0)
    seq = np.clip(seq, 0.0, None)

    s = float(seq.sum())
    maxv = float(seq.max())
    if s < 0.01 or maxv <= 0.0:
        return [1.0] * 512

    scale = min(float(seq.size) / s, 1000.0 / maxv)
    seq *= scale
    seq = np.clip(seq, 0.0, 1000.0)

    s = float(seq.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return [1.0] * 512

    out = [float(x) for x in seq]
    if len(out) == 0:
        return [1.0] * 512
    return out


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400.0, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total = float(max(0.0, budget_s))

    parent = get_parent_sequence()
    if parent is None:
        parent = normalize_sequence(np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64), 512.0)

    if total < 12.0:
        return sanitize_for_return(parent)

    # Leave room for exact validation.
    search_deadline = t0 + max(30.0, total - 95.0)

    pool = []
    try:
        psc = fast_score(parent)
    except Exception:
        psc = float("inf")
    add_candidate(pool, parent, psc)

    global_seq = parent.copy()
    global_score = psc if np.isfinite(psc) else float("inf")

    # Small warm-up / coarse-to-fine phase.
    if total > 800.0:
        warm = ((128, 18.0), (256, 45.0), (512, 160.0))
    elif total > 300.0:
        warm = ((128, 10.0), (256, 30.0), (512, 100.0))
    else:
        warm = ()

    for n, base_b in warm:
        if time.monotonic() >= search_deadline - 260.0:
            break
        b = min(float(base_b), search_deadline - time.monotonic() - 110.0)
        if b < 8.0:
            continue
        try:
            seq, sc = optimize_length(
                n, b, global_seq,
                seed=int(seed) + n,
                random_count=55,
                use_lp=(n <= 512),
            )
        except Exception:
            seq, sc = None, float("inf")
        if seq is not None and np.isfinite(sc):
            add_candidate(pool, seq, sc)
            if sc < global_score:
                global_score = sc
                global_seq = seq

    rem = search_deadline - time.monotonic()

    if total >= 1200.0:
        if rem > 500.0:
            b = min(0.45 * rem, 1000.0)
            try:
                seq, sc = optimize_length(
                    1024, b, global_seq,
                    seed=int(seed) + 1024,
                    random_count=85,
                    use_lp=False,
                )
            except Exception:
                seq, sc = None, float("inf")
            if seq is not None and np.isfinite(sc):
                add_candidate(pool, seq, sc)
                if sc < global_score:
                    global_score = sc
                    global_seq = seq

        rem = search_deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem > 220.0:
            try:
                seq, sc = optimize_length(
                    2048, rem, global_seq,
                    seed=int(seed) + 2048,
                    random_count=100,
                    use_lp=False,
                )
            except Exception:
                seq, sc = None, float("inf")
            if seq is not None and np.isfinite(sc):
                add_candidate(pool, seq, sc)
                if sc < global_score:
                    global_score = sc
                    global_seq = seq

    else:
        if rem > 220.0 and total >= 550.0:
            b = 0.55 * rem
            try:
                seq, sc = optimize_length(
                    1024, b, global_seq,
                    seed=int(seed) + 1024,
                    random_count=80,
                    use_lp=False,
                )
            except Exception:
                seq, sc = None, float("inf")
            if seq is not None and np.isfinite(sc):
                add_candidate(pool, seq, sc)
                if sc < global_score:
                    global_score = sc
                    global_seq = seq

            rem = search_deadline - time.monotonic()

        if rem > 50.0:
            n_final = 1024 if total >= 800.0 else 512
            try:
                seq, sc = optimize_length(
                    n_final, rem, global_seq,
                    seed=int(seed) + 4000,
                    random_count=70,
                    use_lp=(n_final <= 512),
                )
            except Exception:
                seq, sc = None, float("inf")
            if seq is not None and np.isfinite(sc):
                add_candidate(pool, seq, sc)
                if sc < global_score:
                    global_score = sc
                    global_seq = seq

    # Ensure at least one valid fallback candidate.
    fallback = normalize_sequence(np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64), 512.0)
    add_candidate(pool, fallback, fast_score(fallback))

    # Exact validation against the official evaluator.
    exact_deadline = t0 + max(0.0, total) - 12.0
    best_exact_list = sanitize_for_return(global_seq)
    best_exact_score = float("inf")

    # Validate global best first.
    try:
        ex = float(evaluate_sequence(best_exact_list))
        if np.isfinite(ex):
            best_exact_score = ex
    except Exception:
        ex = float("inf")

    top = pool[:12]
    for sc, seq in top:
        if time.monotonic() > exact_deadline:
            break
        lst = sanitize_for_return(seq)
        try:
            ex = float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
        except Exception:
            ex = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(ex) and ex < best_exact_score:
            best_exact_score = ex
            best_exact_list = lst

    if not best_exact_list:
        best_exact_list = sanitize_for_return(fallback)

    return best_exact_list
```
